## UDP round trip above the network's own (µs)

The network's own round trip is ping's between the hosts' data NICs at the start of each state (vm.sh base-rtt, from host a's sender to host b's receiver: min/avg/max). Each mode's round trip less that minimum, per rep: the median across reps, the range in brackets; what the transport, the driver and the clients add over the wire's own round trip, whatever the distance.

## Sustained one-way maximum by message size (M msgs/s)

`flat-out` is the most the publisher sustained with nothing held back: a ceiling, lossy when its loss counters grew. `loss-free` is the highest rate of the ladder below the first point that lost anything in any rep: a loss counter grew on either host, short sends included (those an ARP miss explains aside); a message went unechoed; or a loss read failed or was not taken. The transport's own send-side counters (a full TX ring, an ARP miss, no mbuf, a full kernel send buffer) do not count on their own: each send they refuse is also a short send, and a full ring that queues a vector refuses nothing. Nor does `isolated-nic-irqs`, the data NICs' interrupts on CPUs other than the housekeeping one: a check on the run (under the loss counters' tables), listed with the loss counters but not loss. `keeps up` is the highest rate below the first point where a rep achieved under 98% of the offered rate (saturation, not loss), and `sustainable` the lower of the two.

## Throughput phase frames

From vm.sh counters on both hosts just before and after each flat-out run. Frames are host a's sent (the transport's `pkts` over both ports; the data NICs' `tx_packets` in kernel modes) and host b's received, as rates over the subscriber's warm-up second and samples (`tput_seconds` + 1 s), the only time the publisher has a receiver window. Messages per frame divides the run's msgs/s (over several flows, their aggregate) by the frames sent, so frames sent twice lower it. `tx-queued` (frames a full ring refused and the transport queued to go first) and `tx-eagain` (frames refused back to Aeron) are host a's growth over the run.

## Round trip under open-loop load (µs, from each message's intended send time)

`offered` is the scheduled rate; `achieved` the rate actually offered after warm-up. Below 98% of offered (marked *), the generator or the path could not keep the schedule, and the latencies include that queueing. `echoed` is completed/issued/scheduled; `late, censored` is how often the run fell more than one slot behind, and how many round trips the histogram's bound censored (a max at the bound beside a censored count is a floor, not the slowest round trip). Both are absent (`—`) at a point whose reps predate them. `max send lag` is the longest a measured message waited past its due time to be offered (median of the reps, range in brackets): how far the generator itself fell behind, which is already inside the latencies beside it. `loss counters` lists every loss counter that grew on either host (`sat-` ones are saturation, `noport-` ones frames to a port nobody listens on, such as an endpoint the previous point closed, and `isolated-nic-irqs` the data NICs' interrupts on CPUs other than the housekeeping one, a check on the run: none of them loss).

## Loss by load point (reps that lost anything, of the reps run; NAKs sent)

A rep lost something when a loss counter grew on either host (short sends included, those an ARP miss explains aside; `sat-`, `noport-`, the run-validity counters and the send-side causes on their own aside), a message went unechoed, or a loss read failed or was not taken, as `loss-free` counts it. In brackets, the NAKs both hosts sent over those reps. Compare variants on these counts: one rare event moves the strict `loss-free` rate by whole ladder steps.

| ----------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----- | ----------- | ----------- | ----------- | ----------- | -------- |
| isolated, DPDK                                                          | 288 B | 2/8 (13)    | 0/8         | 2/8 (26)    | 3/8 (13)    | 2/8 (15) |

| isolated, dpdk@AERON_CONDUCTOR_IDLE_STRATEGY_INIT_ARGS=100-10-1us-20us  | 288 B | 0/8         | 0/8         | 4/8 (87)    | 3/8 (35)    | 0/8      |

Data NIC interrupts on CPUs other than the housekeeping one (`isolated-nic-irqs`: on each host, the growth over the run of the data NICs' IRQ counts, under DPDK those of the IRQs the kernel keeps on their PCI addresses, in those CPUs' columns of /proc/interrupts; a check on the run, not loss. vm.sh pins every IRQ to the housekeeping CPU before each driver starts, and the kernel places a NIC's IRQs anew whenever it brings the NIC up or resets it): none of 256 runs grew.

## Aeron's agents and flow control by run (information, never loss)

From Aeron's own counters on each host, read with the loss counters around every run (vm.sh loss). An agent's longest duty cycle counts from its driver's start (one driver a mode and rep), so a run's is the longest up to its end: the highest over reps here. Its cycles over the threshold vm.sh sets (AERON_DRIVER_{CONDUCTOR,SENDER,RECEIVER}_CYCLE_THRESHOLD), the sender's flow-control limits (back-pressure events), the status messages sent and received and the flow control under and over runs are each run's growth, summed over reps. - where a host's rows lack one. None of them is loss.

## Overlaps against chance (1,000 circular shifts of each rep's round trips, seed 1), isolated

Each rep's round trips shifted together by a random offset within its run (its round trips' sum), so
their spacing and every source's own timing stay; p is the share of shifts overlapping at least as many.

| run | source | of the largest round trips | expected by chance | p |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| isolated-dpdk-rtt | host stall | 2 of 80 | 0.01 | 0.001 |
| isolated-dpdk-rtt | ping stall | 28 of 80 | 0.05 | 0.001 |
| isolated-dpdk-rtt | pong stall | 4 of 80 | 0.03 | 0.001 |

## Agent loop gaps by what overlapped them, isolated

Every gap of an agent's loop the DPDK event log stamped (modes with @RUSTERON_DPDK_EVENT_LOG), on the
agent's CPU: a traced preemption; else interrupts there (their time inside the gap); else a gap of the
spare CPU's spinner on the same host (the whole VM paused); else nothing the guest saw: the agent's own
work, or the hypervisor taking this vCPU alone. Lengths in µs: median (largest).

| host | class | gaps | length | interrupt time inside | what |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

The spare CPU's gap spinner, each host, the whole state (vm.sh diag-start; its threshold is the
diag log's threshold_us):

| host | gaps | 10-20 µs | 20-50 µs | 50-100 µs | 100 µs or more | largest |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| a | 1,873 | 0 | 0 | 1,544 | 329 | 3112 |
| b | 2,469 | 0 | 0 | 2,116 | 353 | 5975 |

1,hostcheck,isolated:dpdk@AERON_CONDUCTOR_IDLE_STRATEGY_INIT_ARGS=100-10-1us-20us,a,warning,"T10 warn: SMT is on: on x86 a Kubernetes `cpu: 2` is one physical core, not two, and a busy sibling slows the loop. Measured for ergon-io's SQPOLL thread on the loop's sibling against a core of its own (AWS c7i, arrival → decoded, p50 / p99 / max over three rounds): 1 connection 4.0–4.3 / 7.0–11.7 / 35–46 µs against 3.5–3.8 / 8.4–12.9 / 32–118; 10 connections 7.5–8.4 / 29–33 / 96–349 against 5.2–6.0 / 20–24 / 77–109; 100 connections 7.5–7.8 / 32–34 / 95–112 against 5.0–5.3 / 21–22 / 76–301. Other sibling loads are not measured. Turn SMT off on the NODE. EKS/EC2: launch template `""CpuOptions"": {""CoreCount"": <the type's cores>, ""ThreadsPerCore"": 1}` (not possible on bare metal, or on T2, C7a, M7a, R7a, Apple silicon Mac or Graviton). Self-managed: boot with `nosmt` (`nosmt=force` cannot be undone at runtime), or `echo off > /sys/devices/system/cpu/smt/control` before the kubelet starts, then delete its cpu_manager_state so it re-pins. Azure: a size without SMT (Fasv6, Falsv6, Famsv6, Dpsv6, Dplsv6: one vCPU is one physical core) (evidence: git show 128747b9^:io/benches/results/flags-aws-20261015T122127Z/tables.md (lines 174-279, arrival → decoded, three rounds); AWS instance-optimize-cpu and instance-specify-cpu-options (CpuOptions ThreadsPerCore); kernel-parameters.txt (nosmt); ABI/testing/sysfs-devices-system-cpu (smt/active, smt/control); Azure compute-optimized Fasv6/Falsv6/Famsv6 and general-purpose Dpsv6/Dplsv6 (no SMT)) (ERGON_HOST_CHECKS=off silences host-check warnings)"

1,hostcheck,isolated:dpdk,a,warning,"T10 warn: SMT is on: on x86 a Kubernetes `cpu: 2` is one physical core, not two, and a busy sibling slows the loop. Measured for ergon-io's SQPOLL thread on the loop's sibling against a core of its own (AWS c7i, arrival → decoded, p50 / p99 / max over three rounds): 1 connection 4.0–4.3 / 7.0–11.7 / 35–46 µs against 3.5–3.8 / 8.4–12.9 / 32–118; 10 connections 7.5–8.4 / 29–33 / 96–349 against 5.2–6.0 / 20–24 / 77–109; 100 connections 7.5–7.8 / 32–34 / 95–112 against 5.0–5.3 / 21–22 / 76–301. Other sibling loads are not measured. Turn SMT off on the NODE. EKS/EC2: launch template `""CpuOptions"": {""CoreCount"": <the type's cores>, ""ThreadsPerCore"": 1}` (not possible on bare metal, or on T2, C7a, M7a, R7a, Apple silicon Mac or Graviton). Self-managed: boot with `nosmt` (`nosmt=force` cannot be undone at runtime), or `echo off > /sys/devices/system/cpu/smt/control` before the kubelet starts, then delete its cpu_manager_state so it re-pins. Azure: a size without SMT (Fasv6, Falsv6, Famsv6, Dpsv6, Dplsv6: one vCPU is one physical core) (evidence: git show 128747b9^:io/benches/results/flags-aws-20261015T122127Z/tables.md (lines 174-279, arrival → decoded, three rounds); AWS instance-optimize-cpu and instance-specify-cpu-options (CpuOptions ThreadsPerCore); kernel-parameters.txt (nosmt); ABI/testing/sysfs-devices-system-cpu (smt/active, smt/control); Azure compute-optimized Fasv6/Falsv6/Famsv6 and general-purpose Dpsv6/Dplsv6 (no SMT)) (ERGON_HOST_CHECKS=off silences host-check warnings)"

1,hostcheck,isolated:dpdk@THP=never,a,warning,"T10 warn: SMT is on: on x86 a Kubernetes `cpu: 2` is one physical core, not two, and a busy sibling slows the loop. Measured for ergon-io's SQPOLL thread on the loop's sibling against a core of its own (AWS c7i, arrival → decoded, p50 / p99 / max over three rounds): 1 connection 4.0–4.3 / 7.0–11.7 / 35–46 µs against 3.5–3.8 / 8.4–12.9 / 32–118; 10 connections 7.5–8.4 / 29–33 / 96–349 against 5.2–6.0 / 20–24 / 77–109; 100 connections 7.5–7.8 / 32–34 / 95–112 against 5.0–5.3 / 21–22 / 76–301. Other sibling loads are not measured. Turn SMT off on the NODE. EKS/EC2: launch template `""CpuOptions"": {""CoreCount"": <the type's cores>, ""ThreadsPerCore"": 1}` (not possible on bare metal, or on T2, C7a, M7a, R7a, Apple silicon Mac or Graviton). Self-managed: boot with `nosmt` (`nosmt=force` cannot be undone at runtime), or `echo off > /sys/devices/system/cpu/smt/control` before the kubelet starts, then delete its cpu_manager_state so it re-pins. Azure: a size without SMT (Fasv6, Falsv6, Famsv6, Dpsv6, Dplsv6: one vCPU is one physical core) (evidence: git show 128747b9^:io/benches/results/flags-aws-20261015T122127Z/tables.md (lines 174-279, arrival → decoded, three rounds); AWS instance-optimize-cpu and instance-specify-cpu-options (CpuOptions ThreadsPerCore); kernel-parameters.txt (nosmt); ABI/testing/sysfs-devices-system-cpu (smt/active, smt/control); Azure compute-optimized Fasv6/Falsv6/Famsv6 and general-purpose Dpsv6/Dplsv6 (no SMT)) (ERGON_HOST_CHECKS=off silences host-check warnings)"

Measured for ergon-io's SQPOLL thread on the loop's sibling against a core of its own (AWS c7i, arrival → decoded, p50 / p99 / max over three rounds): 1 connection 4.0–4.3 / 7.0–11.7 / 35–46 µs against 3.5–3.8 / 8.4–12.9 / 32–118; 10 connections 7.5–8.4 / 29–33 / 96–349 against 5.2–6.0 / 20–24 / 77–109; 100 connections 7.5–7.8 / 32–34 / 95–112 against 5.0–5.3 / 21–22 / 76–301. Other sibling loads are not measured. Turn SMT off on the NODE. EKS/EC2: launch template `""CpuOptions"": {""CoreCount"": <the type's cores>, ""ThreadsPerCore"": 1}` (not possible on bare metal, or on T2, C7a, M7a, R7a, Apple silicon Mac or Graviton). Self-managed: boot with `nosmt` (`nosmt=force` cannot be undone at runtime), or `echo off > /sys/devices/system/cpu/smt/control` before the kubelet starts, then delete its cpu_manager_state so it re-pins. Azure: a size without SMT (Fasv6, Falsv6, Famsv6, Dpsv6, Dplsv6: one vCPU is one physical core) (evidence: git show 128747b9^:io/benches/results/flags-aws-20261015T122127Z/tables.md (lines 174-279, arrival → decoded, three rounds); AWS instance-optimize-cpu and instance-specify-cpu-options (CpuOptions ThreadsPerCore); kernel-parameters.txt (nosmt); ABI/testing/sysfs-devices-system-cpu (smt/active, smt/control); Azure compute-optimized Fasv6/Falsv6/Famsv6 and general-purpose Dpsv6/Dplsv6 (no SMT)) (ERGON_HOST_CHECKS=off silences host-check warnings)"

Measured for ergon-io's SQPOLL thread on the loop's sibling against a core of its own (AWS c7i, arrival → decoded, p50 / p99 / max over three rounds): 1 connection 4.0–4.3 / 7.0–11.7 / 35–46 µs against 3.5–3.8 / 8.4–12.9 / 32–118; 10 connections 7.5–8.4 / 29–33 / 96–349 against 5.2–6.0 / 20–24 / 77–109; 100 connections 7.5–7.8 / 32–34 / 95–112 against 5.0–5.3 / 21–22 / 76–301. Other sibling loads are not measured. Turn SMT off on the NODE. EKS/EC2: launch template `""CpuOptions"": {""CoreCount"": <the type's cores>, ""ThreadsPerCore"": 1}` (not possible on bare metal, or on T2, C7a, M7a, R7a, Apple silicon Mac or Graviton). Self-managed: boot with `nosmt` (`nosmt=force` cannot be undone at runtime), or `echo off > /sys/devices/system/cpu/smt/control` before the kubelet starts, then delete its cpu_manager_state so it re-pins. Azure: a size without SMT (Fasv6, Falsv6, Famsv6, Dpsv6, Dplsv6: one vCPU is one physical core) (evidence: git show 128747b9^:io/benches/results/flags-aws-20261015T122127Z/tables.md (lines 174-279, arrival → decoded, three rounds); AWS instance-optimize-cpu and instance-specify-cpu-options (CpuOptions ThreadsPerCore); kernel-parameters.txt (nosmt); ABI/testing/sysfs-devices-system-cpu (smt/active, smt/control); Azure compute-optimized Fasv6/Falsv6/Famsv6 and general-purpose Dpsv6/Dplsv6 (no SMT)) (ERGON_HOST_CHECKS=off silences host-check warnings)"

Measured for ergon-io's SQPOLL thread on the loop's sibling against a core of its own (AWS c7i, arrival → decoded, p50 / p99 / max over three rounds): 1 connection 4.0–4.3 / 7.0–11.7 / 35–46 µs against 3.5–3.8 / 8.4–12.9 / 32–118; 10 connections 7.5–8.4 / 29–33 / 96–349 against 5.2–6.0 / 20–24 / 77–109; 100 connections 7.5–7.8 / 32–34 / 95–112 against 5.0–5.3 / 21–22 / 76–301. Other sibling loads are not measured. Turn SMT off on the NODE. EKS/EC2: launch template `""CpuOptions"": {""CoreCount"": <the type's cores>, ""ThreadsPerCore"": 1}` (not possible on bare metal, or on T2, C7a, M7a, R7a, Apple silicon Mac or Graviton). Self-managed: boot with `nosmt` (`nosmt=force` cannot be undone at runtime), or `echo off > /sys/devices/system/cpu/smt/control` before the kubelet starts, then delete its cpu_manager_state so it re-pins. Azure: a size without SMT (Fasv6, Falsv6, Famsv6, Dpsv6, Dplsv6: one vCPU is one physical core) (evidence: git show 128747b9^:io/benches/results/flags-aws-20261015T122127Z/tables.md (lines 174-279, arrival → decoded, three rounds); AWS instance-optimize-cpu and instance-specify-cpu-options (CpuOptions ThreadsPerCore); kernel-parameters.txt (nosmt); ABI/testing/sysfs-devices-system-cpu (smt/active, smt/control); Azure compute-optimized Fasv6/Falsv6/Famsv6 and general-purpose Dpsv6/Dplsv6 (no SMT)) (ERGON_HOST_CHECKS=off silences host-check warnings)"

Measured for ergon-io's SQPOLL thread on the loop's sibling against a core of its own (AWS c7i, arrival → decoded, p50 / p99 / max over three rounds): 1 connection 4.0–4.3 / 7.0–11.7 / 35–46 µs against 3.5–3.8 / 8.4–12.9 / 32–118; 10 connections 7.5–8.4 / 29–33 / 96–349 against 5.2–6.0 / 20–24 / 77–109; 100 connections 7.5–7.8 / 32–34 / 95–112 against 5.0–5.3 / 21–22 / 76–301. Other sibling loads are not measured. Turn SMT off on the NODE. EKS/EC2: launch template `""CpuOptions"": {""CoreCount"": <the type's cores>, ""ThreadsPerCore"": 1}` (not possible on bare metal, or on T2, C7a, M7a, R7a, Apple silicon Mac or Graviton). Self-managed: boot with `nosmt` (`nosmt=force` cannot be undone at runtime), or `echo off > /sys/devices/system/cpu/smt/control` before the kubelet starts, then delete its cpu_manager_state so it re-pins. Azure: a size without SMT (Fasv6, Falsv6, Famsv6, Dpsv6, Dplsv6: one vCPU is one physical core) (evidence: git show 128747b9^:io/benches/results/flags-aws-20261015T122127Z/tables.md (lines 174-279, arrival → decoded, three rounds); AWS instance-optimize-cpu and instance-specify-cpu-options (CpuOptions ThreadsPerCore); kernel-parameters.txt (nosmt); ABI/testing/sysfs-devices-system-cpu (smt/active, smt/control); Azure compute-optimized Fasv6/Falsv6/Famsv6 and general-purpose Dpsv6/Dplsv6 (no SMT)) (ERGON_HOST_CHECKS=off silences host-check warnings)"

Measured for ergon-io's SQPOLL thread on the loop's sibling against a core of its own (AWS c7i, arrival → decoded, p50 / p99 / max over three rounds): 1 connection 4.0–4.3 / 7.0–11.7 / 35–46 µs against 3.5–3.8 / 8.4–12.9 / 32–118; 10 connections 7.5–8.4 / 29–33 / 96–349 against 5.2–6.0 / 20–24 / 77–109; 100 connections 7.5–7.8 / 32–34 / 95–112 against 5.0–5.3 / 21–22 / 76–301. Other sibling loads are not measured. Turn SMT off on the NODE. EKS/EC2: launch template `""CpuOptions"": {""CoreCount"": <the type's cores>, ""ThreadsPerCore"": 1}` (not possible on bare metal, or on T2, C7a, M7a, R7a, Apple silicon Mac or Graviton). Self-managed: boot with `nosmt` (`nosmt=force` cannot be undone at runtime), or `echo off > /sys/devices/system/cpu/smt/control` before the kubelet starts, then delete its cpu_manager_state so it re-pins. Azure: a size without SMT (Fasv6, Falsv6, Famsv6, Dpsv6, Dplsv6: one vCPU is one physical core) (evidence: git show 128747b9^:io/benches/results/flags-aws-20261015T122127Z/tables.md (lines 174-279, arrival → decoded, three rounds); AWS instance-optimize-cpu and instance-specify-cpu-options (CpuOptions ThreadsPerCore); kernel-parameters.txt (nosmt); ABI/testing/sysfs-devices-system-cpu (smt/active, smt/control); Azure compute-optimized Fasv6/Falsv6/Famsv6 and general-purpose Dpsv6/Dplsv6 (no SMT)) (ERGON_HOST_CHECKS=off silences host-check warnings)"

Measured for ergon-io's SQPOLL thread on the loop's sibling against a core of its own (AWS c7i, arrival → decoded, p50 / p99 / max over three rounds): 1 connection 4.0–4.3 / 7.0–11.7 / 35–46 µs against 3.5–3.8 / 8.4–12.9 / 32–118; 10 connections 7.5–8.4 / 29–33 / 96–349 against 5.2–6.0 / 20–24 / 77–109; 100 connections 7.5–7.8 / 32–34 / 95–112 against 5.0–5.3 / 21–22 / 76–301. Self-managed: boot with `nosmt` (`nosmt=force` cannot be undone at runtime), or `echo off > /sys/devices/system/cpu/smt/control` before the kubelet starts, then delete its cpu_manager_state so it re-pins. Azure: a size without SMT (Fasv6, Falsv6, Famsv6, Dpsv6, Dplsv6: one vCPU is one physical core) (evidence: git show 128747b9^:io/benches/results/flags-aws-20261015T122127Z/tables.md (lines 174-279, arrival → decoded, three rounds); AWS instance-optimize-cpu and instance-specify-cpu-options (CpuOptions ThreadsPerCore); kernel-parameters.txt (nosmt); ABI/testing/sysfs-devices-system-cpu (smt/active, smt/control); Azure compute-optimized Fasv6/Falsv6/Famsv6 and general-purpose Dpsv6/Dplsv6 (no SMT)) (ERGON_HOST_CHECKS=off silences host-check warnings)"

3,hostcheck,isolated:dpdk@AERON_CONDUCTOR_IDLE_STRATEGY_INIT_ARGS=100-10-1us-20us,a,warning,"T10 warn: SMT is on: on x86 a Kubernetes `cpu: 2` is one physical core, not two, and a busy sibling slows the loop. Measured for ergon-io's SQPOLL thread on the loop's sibling against a core of its own (AWS c7i, arrival → decoded, p50 / p99 / max over three rounds): 1 connection 4.0–4.3 / 7.0–11.7 / 35–46 µs against 3.5–3.8 / 8.4–12.9 / 32–118; 10 connections 7.5–8.4 / 29–33 / 96–349 against 5.2–6.0 / 20–24 / 77–109; 100 connections 7.5–7.8 / 32–34 / 95–112 against 5.0–5.3 / 21–22 / 76–301. Other sibling loads are not measured. Turn SMT off on the NODE. EKS/EC2: launch template `""CpuOptions"": {""CoreCount"": <the type's cores>, ""ThreadsPerCore"": 1}` (not possible on bare metal, or on T2, C7a, M7a, R7a, Apple silicon Mac or Graviton). Self-managed: boot with `nosmt` (`nosmt=force` cannot be undone at runtime), or `echo off > /sys/devices/system/cpu/smt/control` before the kubelet starts, then delete its cpu_manager_state so it re-pins. Azure: a size without SMT (Fasv6, Falsv6, Famsv6, Dpsv6, Dplsv6: one vCPU is one physical core) (evidence: git show 128747b9^:io/benches/results/flags-aws-20261015T122127Z/tables.md (lines 174-279, arrival → decoded, three rounds); AWS instance-optimize-cpu and instance-specify-cpu-options (CpuOptions ThreadsPerCore); kernel-parameters.txt (nosmt); ABI/testing/sysfs-devices-system-cpu (smt/active, smt/control); Azure compute-optimized Fasv6/Falsv6/Famsv6 and general-purpose Dpsv6/Dplsv6 (no SMT)) (ERGON_HOST_CHECKS=off silences host-check warnings)"

3,hostcheck,isolated:dpdk,a,warning,"T10 warn: SMT is on: on x86 a Kubernetes `cpu: 2` is one physical core, not two, and a busy sibling slows the loop. Measured for ergon-io's SQPOLL thread on the loop's sibling against a core of its own (AWS c7i, arrival → decoded, p50 / p99 / max over three rounds): 1 connection 4.0–4.3 / 7.0–11.7 / 35–46 µs against 3.5–3.8 / 8.4–12.9 / 32–118; 10 connections 7.5–8.4 / 29–33 / 96–349 against 5.2–6.0 / 20–24 / 77–109; 100 connections 7.5–7.8 / 32–34 / 95–112 against 5.0–5.3 / 21–22 / 76–301. Other sibling loads are not measured. Turn SMT off on the NODE. EKS/EC2: launch template `""CpuOptions"": {""CoreCount"": <the type's cores>, ""ThreadsPerCore"": 1}` (not possible on bare metal, or on T2, C7a, M7a, R7a, Apple silicon Mac or Graviton). Self-managed: boot with `nosmt` (`nosmt=force` cannot be undone at runtime), or `echo off > /sys/devices/system/cpu/smt/control` before the kubelet starts, then delete its cpu_manager_state so it re-pins. Azure: a size without SMT (Fasv6, Falsv6, Famsv6, Dpsv6, Dplsv6: one vCPU is one physical core) (evidence: git show 128747b9^:io/benches/results/flags-aws-20261015T122127Z/tables.md (lines 174-279, arrival → decoded, three rounds); AWS instance-optimize-cpu and instance-specify-cpu-options (CpuOptions ThreadsPerCore); kernel-parameters.txt (nosmt); ABI/testing/sysfs-devices-system-cpu (smt/active, smt/control); Azure compute-optimized Fasv6/Falsv6/Famsv6 and general-purpose Dpsv6/Dplsv6 (no SMT)) (ERGON_HOST_CHECKS=off silences host-check warnings)"

4,hostcheck,isolated:dpdk,a,warning,"T10 warn: SMT is on: on x86 a Kubernetes `cpu: 2` is one physical core, not two, and a busy sibling slows the loop. Measured for ergon-io's SQPOLL thread on the loop's sibling against a core of its own (AWS c7i, arrival → decoded, p50 / p99 / max over three rounds): 1 connection 4.0–4.3 / 7.0–11.7 / 35–46 µs against 3.5–3.8 / 8.4–12.9 / 32–118; 10 connections 7.5–8.4 / 29–33 / 96–349 against 5.2–6.0 / 20–24 / 77–109; 100 connections 7.5–7.8 / 32–34 / 95–112 against 5.0–5.3 / 21–22 / 76–301. Other sibling loads are not measured. Turn SMT off on the NODE. EKS/EC2: launch template `""CpuOptions"": {""CoreCount"": <the type's cores>, ""ThreadsPerCore"": 1}` (not possible on bare metal, or on T2, C7a, M7a, R7a, Apple silicon Mac or Graviton). Self-managed: boot with `nosmt` (`nosmt=force` cannot be undone at runtime), or `echo off > /sys/devices/system/cpu/smt/control` before the kubelet starts, then delete its cpu_manager_state so it re-pins. Azure: a size without SMT (Fasv6, Falsv6, Famsv6, Dpsv6, Dplsv6: one vCPU is one physical core) (evidence: git show 128747b9^:io/benches/results/flags-aws-20261015T122127Z/tables.md (lines 174-279, arrival → decoded, three rounds); AWS instance-optimize-cpu and instance-specify-cpu-options (CpuOptions ThreadsPerCore); kernel-parameters.txt (nosmt); ABI/testing/sysfs-devices-system-cpu (smt/active, smt/control); Azure compute-optimized Fasv6/Falsv6/Famsv6 and general-purpose Dpsv6/Dplsv6 (no SMT)) (ERGON_HOST_CHECKS=off silences host-check warnings)"

Measured for ergon-io's SQPOLL thread on the loop's sibling against a core of its own (AWS c7i, arrival → decoded, p50 / p99 / max over three rounds): 1 connection 4.0–4.3 / 7.0–11.7 / 35–46 µs against 3.5–3.8 / 8.4–12.9 / 32–118; 10 connections 7.5–8.4 / 29–33 / 96–349 against 5.2–6.0 / 20–24 / 77–109; 100 connections 7.5–7.8 / 32–34 / 95–112 against 5.0–5.3 / 21–22 / 76–301. Other sibling loads are not measured. Turn SMT off on the NODE. EKS/EC2: launch template `""CpuOptions"": {""CoreCount"": <the type's cores>, ""ThreadsPerCore"": 1}` (not possible on bare metal, or on T2, C7a, M7a, R7a, Apple silicon Mac or Graviton). Self-managed: boot with `nosmt` (`nosmt=force` cannot be undone at runtime), or `echo off > /sys/devices/system/cpu/smt/control` before the kubelet starts, then delete its cpu_manager_state so it re-pins. Azure: a size without SMT (Fasv6, Falsv6, Famsv6, Dpsv6, Dplsv6: one vCPU is one physical core) (evidence: git show 128747b9^:io/benches/results/flags-aws-20261015T122127Z/tables.md (lines 174-279, arrival → decoded, three rounds); AWS instance-optimize-cpu and instance-specify-cpu-options (CpuOptions ThreadsPerCore); kernel-parameters.txt (nosmt); ABI/testing/sysfs-devices-system-cpu (smt/active, smt/control); Azure compute-optimized Fasv6/Falsv6/Famsv6 and general-purpose Dpsv6/Dplsv6 (no SMT)) (ERGON_HOST_CHECKS=off silences host-check warnings)"

Measured for ergon-io's SQPOLL thread on the loop's sibling against a core of its own (AWS c7i, arrival → decoded, p50 / p99 / max over three rounds): 1 connection 4.0–4.3 / 7.0–11.7 / 35–46 µs against 3.5–3.8 / 8.4–12.9 / 32–118; 10 connections 7.5–8.4 / 29–33 / 96–349 against 5.2–6.0 / 20–24 / 77–109; 100 connections 7.5–7.8 / 32–34 / 95–112 against 5.0–5.3 / 21–22 / 76–301. Other sibling loads are not measured. Turn SMT off on the NODE. EKS/EC2: launch template `""CpuOptions"": {""CoreCount"": <the type's cores>, ""ThreadsPerCore"": 1}` (not possible on bare metal, or on T2, C7a, M7a, R7a, Apple silicon Mac or Graviton). Self-managed: boot with `nosmt` (`nosmt=force` cannot be undone at runtime), or `echo off > /sys/devices/system/cpu/smt/control` before the kubelet starts, then delete its cpu_manager_state so it re-pins. Azure: a size without SMT (Fasv6, Falsv6, Famsv6, Dpsv6, Dplsv6: one vCPU is one physical core) (evidence: git show 128747b9^:io/benches/results/flags-aws-20261015T122127Z/tables.md (lines 174-279, arrival → decoded, three rounds); AWS instance-optimize-cpu and instance-specify-cpu-options (CpuOptions ThreadsPerCore); kernel-parameters.txt (nosmt); ABI/testing/sysfs-devices-system-cpu (smt/active, smt/control); Azure compute-optimized Fasv6/Falsv6/Famsv6 and general-purpose Dpsv6/Dplsv6 (no SMT)) (ERGON_HOST_CHECKS=off silences host-check warnings)"

5,hostcheck,isolated:dpdk,a,warning,"T10 warn: SMT is on: on x86 a Kubernetes `cpu: 2` is one physical core, not two, and a busy sibling slows the loop. Measured for ergon-io's SQPOLL thread on the loop's sibling against a core of its own (AWS c7i, arrival → decoded, p50 / p99 / max over three rounds): 1 connection 4.0–4.3 / 7.0–11.7 / 35–46 µs against 3.5–3.8 / 8.4–12.9 / 32–118; 10 connections 7.5–8.4 / 29–33 / 96–349 against 5.2–6.0 / 20–24 / 77–109; 100 connections 7.5–7.8 / 32–34 / 95–112 against 5.0–5.3 / 21–22 / 76–301. Other sibling loads are not measured. Turn SMT off on the NODE. EKS/EC2: launch template `""CpuOptions"": {""CoreCount"": <the type's cores>, ""ThreadsPerCore"": 1}` (not possible on bare metal, or on T2, C7a, M7a, R7a, Apple silicon Mac or Graviton). Self-managed: boot with `nosmt` (`nosmt=force` cannot be undone at runtime), or `echo off > /sys/devices/system/cpu/smt/control` before the kubelet starts, then delete its cpu_manager_state so it re-pins. Azure: a size without SMT (Fasv6, Falsv6, Famsv6, Dpsv6, Dplsv6: one vCPU is one physical core) (evidence: git show 128747b9^:io/benches/results/flags-aws-20261015T122127Z/tables.md (lines 174-279, arrival → decoded, three rounds); AWS instance-optimize-cpu and instance-specify-cpu-options (CpuOptions ThreadsPerCore); kernel-parameters.txt (nosmt); ABI/testing/sysfs-devices-system-cpu (smt/active, smt/control); Azure compute-optimized Fasv6/Falsv6/Famsv6 and general-purpose Dpsv6/Dplsv6 (no SMT)) (ERGON_HOST_CHECKS=off silences host-check warnings)"

5,hostcheck,isolated:dpdk@AERON_CONDUCTOR_IDLE_STRATEGY_INIT_ARGS=100-10-1us-20us,a,warning,"T10 warn: SMT is on: on x86 a Kubernetes `cpu: 2` is one physical core, not two, and a busy sibling slows the loop. Measured for ergon-io's SQPOLL thread on the loop's sibling against a core of its own (AWS c7i, arrival → decoded, p50 / p99 / max over three rounds): 1 connection 4.0–4.3 / 7.0–11.7 / 35–46 µs against 3.5–3.8 / 8.4–12.9 / 32–118; 10 connections 7.5–8.4 / 29–33 / 96–349 against 5.2–6.0 / 20–24 / 77–109; 100 connections 7.5–7.8 / 32–34 / 95–112 against 5.0–5.3 / 21–22 / 76–301. Other sibling loads are not measured. Turn SMT off on the NODE. EKS/EC2: launch template `""CpuOptions"": {""CoreCount"": <the type's cores>, ""ThreadsPerCore"": 1}` (not possible on bare metal, or on T2, C7a, M7a, R7a, Apple silicon Mac or Graviton). Self-managed: boot with `nosmt` (`nosmt=force` cannot be undone at runtime), or `echo off > /sys/devices/system/cpu/smt/control` before the kubelet starts, then delete its cpu_manager_state so it re-pins. Azure: a size without SMT (Fasv6, Falsv6, Famsv6, Dpsv6, Dplsv6: one vCPU is one physical core) (evidence: git show 128747b9^:io/benches/results/flags-aws-20261015T122127Z/tables.md (lines 174-279, arrival → decoded, three rounds); AWS instance-optimize-cpu and instance-specify-cpu-options (CpuOptions ThreadsPerCore); kernel-parameters.txt (nosmt); ABI/testing/sysfs-devices-system-cpu (smt/active, smt/control); Azure compute-optimized Fasv6/Falsv6/Famsv6 and general-purpose Dpsv6/Dplsv6 (no SMT)) (ERGON_HOST_CHECKS=off silences host-check warnings)"

Self-managed: boot with `nosmt` (`nosmt=force` cannot be undone at runtime), or `echo off > /sys/devices/system/cpu/smt/control` before the kubelet starts, then delete its cpu_manager_state so it re-pins. Measured for ergon-io's SQPOLL thread on the loop's sibling against a core of its own (AWS c7i, arrival → decoded, p50 / p99 / max over three rounds): 1 connection 4.0–4.3 / 7.0–11.7 / 35–46 µs against 3.5–3.8 / 8.4–12.9 / 32–118; 10 connections 7.5–8.4 / 29–33 / 96–349 against 5.2–6.0 / 20–24 / 77–109; 100 connections 7.5–7.8 / 32–34 / 95–112 against 5.0–5.3 / 21–22 / 76–301. Other sibling loads are not measured. Turn SMT off on the NODE. EKS/EC2: launch template `""CpuOptions"": {""CoreCount"": <the type's cores>, ""ThreadsPerCore"": 1}` (not possible on bare metal, or on T2, C7a, M7a, R7a, Apple silicon Mac or Graviton). Self-managed: boot with `nosmt` (`nosmt=force` cannot be undone at runtime), or `echo off > /sys/devices/system/cpu/smt/control` before the kubelet starts, then delete its cpu_manager_state so it re-pins. Azure: a size without SMT (Fasv6, Falsv6, Famsv6, Dpsv6, Dplsv6: one vCPU is one physical core) (evidence: git show 128747b9^:io/benches/results/flags-aws-20261015T122127Z/tables.md (lines 174-279, arrival → decoded, three rounds); AWS instance-optimize-cpu and instance-specify-cpu-options (CpuOptions ThreadsPerCore); kernel-parameters.txt (nosmt); ABI/testing/sysfs-devices-system-cpu (smt/active, smt/control); Azure compute-optimized Fasv6/Falsv6/Famsv6 and general-purpose Dpsv6/Dplsv6 (no SMT)) (ERGON_HOST_CHECKS=off silences host-check warnings)"

6,hostcheck,isolated:dpdk@THP=never,a,warning,"T10 warn: SMT is on: on x86 a Kubernetes `cpu: 2` is one physical core, not two, and a busy sibling slows the loop. Measured for ergon-io's SQPOLL thread on the loop's sibling against a core of its own (AWS c7i, arrival → decoded, p50 / p99 / max over three rounds): 1 connection 4.0–4.3 / 7.0–11.7 / 35–46 µs against 3.5–3.8 / 8.4–12.9 / 32–118; 10 connections 7.5–8.4 / 29–33 / 96–349 against 5.2–6.0 / 20–24 / 77–109; 100 connections 7.5–7.8 / 32–34 / 95–112 against 5.0–5.3 / 21–22 / 76–301. Other sibling loads are not measured. Turn SMT off on the NODE. EKS/EC2: launch template `""CpuOptions"": {""CoreCount"": <the type's cores>, ""ThreadsPerCore"": 1}` (not possible on bare metal, or on T2, C7a, M7a, R7a, Apple silicon Mac or Graviton). Self-managed: boot with `nosmt` (`nosmt=force` cannot be undone at runtime), or `echo off > /sys/devices/system/cpu/smt/control` before the kubelet starts, then delete its cpu_manager_state so it re-pins. Azure: a size without SMT (Fasv6, Falsv6, Famsv6, Dpsv6, Dplsv6: one vCPU is one physical core) (evidence: git show 128747b9^:io/benches/results/flags-aws-20261015T122127Z/tables.md (lines 174-279, arrival → decoded, three rounds); AWS instance-optimize-cpu and instance-specify-cpu-options (CpuOptions ThreadsPerCore); kernel-parameters.txt (nosmt); ABI/testing/sysfs-devices-system-cpu (smt/active, smt/control); Azure compute-optimized Fasv6/Falsv6/Famsv6 and general-purpose Dpsv6/Dplsv6 (no SMT)) (ERGON_HOST_CHECKS=off silences host-check warnings)"

Other sibling loads are not measured. Turn SMT off on the NODE. Self-managed: boot with `nosmt` (`nosmt=force` cannot be undone at runtime), or `echo off > /sys/devices/system/cpu/smt/control` before the kubelet starts, then delete its cpu_manager_state so it re-pins. Azure: a size without SMT (Fasv6, Falsv6, Famsv6, Dpsv6, Dplsv6: one vCPU is one physical core) (evidence: git show 128747b9^:io/benches/results/flags-aws-20261015T122127Z/tables.md (lines 174-279, arrival → decoded, three rounds); AWS instance-optimize-cpu and instance-specify-cpu-options (CpuOptions ThreadsPerCore); kernel-parameters.txt (nosmt); ABI/testing/sysfs-devices-system-cpu (smt/active, smt/control); Azure compute-optimized Fasv6/Falsv6/Famsv6 and general-purpose Dpsv6/Dplsv6 (no SMT)) (ERGON_HOST_CHECKS=off silences host-check warnings)"

Measured for ergon-io's SQPOLL thread on the loop's sibling against a core of its own (AWS c7i, arrival → decoded, p50 / p99 / max over three rounds): 1 connection 4.0–4.3 / 7.0–11.7 / 35–46 µs against 3.5–3.8 / 8.4–12.9 / 32–118; 10 connections 7.5–8.4 / 29–33 / 96–349 against 5.2–6.0 / 20–24 / 77–109; 100 connections 7.5–7.8 / 32–34 / 95–112 against 5.0–5.3 / 21–22 / 76–301. Other sibling loads are not measured. Turn SMT off on the NODE. EKS/EC2: launch template `""CpuOptions"": {""CoreCount"": <the type's cores>, ""ThreadsPerCore"": 1}` (not possible on bare metal, or on T2, C7a, M7a, R7a, Apple silicon Mac or Graviton). Self-managed: boot with `nosmt` (`nosmt=force` cannot be undone at runtime), or `echo off > /sys/devices/system/cpu/smt/control` before the kubelet starts, then delete its cpu_manager_state so it re-pins. Azure: a size without SMT (Fasv6, Falsv6, Famsv6, Dpsv6, Dplsv6: one vCPU is one physical core) (evidence: git show 128747b9^:io/benches/results/flags-aws-20261015T122127Z/tables.md (lines 174-279, arrival → decoded, three rounds); AWS instance-optimize-cpu and instance-specify-cpu-options (CpuOptions ThreadsPerCore); kernel-parameters.txt (nosmt); ABI/testing/sysfs-devices-system-cpu (smt/active, smt/control); Azure compute-optimized Fasv6/Falsv6/Famsv6 and general-purpose Dpsv6/Dplsv6 (no SMT)) (ERGON_HOST_CHECKS=off silences host-check warnings)"

Measured for ergon-io's SQPOLL thread on the loop's sibling against a core of its own (AWS c7i, arrival → decoded, p50 / p99 / max over three rounds): 1 connection 4.0–4.3 / 7.0–11.7 / 35–46 µs against 3.5–3.8 / 8.4–12.9 / 32–118; 10 connections 7.5–8.4 / 29–33 / 96–349 against 5.2–6.0 / 20–24 / 77–109; 100 connections 7.5–7.8 / 32–34 / 95–112 against 5.0–5.3 / 21–22 / 76–301. Other sibling loads are not measured. Turn SMT off on the NODE. EKS/EC2: launch template `""CpuOptions"": {""CoreCount"": <the type's cores>, ""ThreadsPerCore"": 1}` (not possible on bare metal, or on T2, C7a, M7a, R7a, Apple silicon Mac or Graviton). Self-managed: boot with `nosmt` (`nosmt=force` cannot be undone at runtime), or `echo off > /sys/devices/system/cpu/smt/control` before the kubelet starts, then delete its cpu_manager_state so it re-pins. Azure: a size without SMT (Fasv6, Falsv6, Famsv6, Dpsv6, Dplsv6: one vCPU is one physical core) (evidence: git show 128747b9^:io/benches/results/flags-aws-20261015T122127Z/tables.md (lines 174-279, arrival → decoded, three rounds); AWS instance-optimize-cpu and instance-specify-cpu-options (CpuOptions ThreadsPerCore); kernel-parameters.txt (nosmt); ABI/testing/sysfs-devices-system-cpu (smt/active, smt/control); Azure compute-optimized Fasv6/Falsv6/Famsv6 and general-purpose Dpsv6/Dplsv6 (no SMT)) (ERGON_HOST_CHECKS=off silences host-check warnings)"

6,hostcheck,isolated:dpdk,a,warning,"T10 warn: SMT is on: on x86 a Kubernetes `cpu: 2` is one physical core, not two, and a busy sibling slows the loop. Measured for ergon-io's SQPOLL thread on the loop's sibling against a core of its own (AWS c7i, arrival → decoded, p50 / p99 / max over three rounds): 1 connection 4.0–4.3 / 7.0–11.7 / 35–46 µs against 3.5–3.8 / 8.4–12.9 / 32–118; 10 connections 7.5–8.4 / 29–33 / 96–349 against 5.2–6.0 / 20–24 / 77–109; 100 connections 7.5–7.8 / 32–34 / 95–112 against 5.0–5.3 / 21–22 / 76–301. Other sibling loads are not measured. Turn SMT off on the NODE. EKS/EC2: launch template `""CpuOptions"": {""CoreCount"": <the type's cores>, ""ThreadsPerCore"": 1}` (not possible on bare metal, or on T2, C7a, M7a, R7a, Apple silicon Mac or Graviton). Self-managed: boot with `nosmt` (`nosmt=force` cannot be undone at runtime), or `echo off > /sys/devices/system/cpu/smt/control` before the kubelet starts, then delete its cpu_manager_state so it re-pins. Azure: a size without SMT (Fasv6, Falsv6, Famsv6, Dpsv6, Dplsv6: one vCPU is one physical core) (evidence: git show 128747b9^:io/benches/results/flags-aws-20261015T122127Z/tables.md (lines 174-279, arrival → decoded, three rounds); AWS instance-optimize-cpu and instance-specify-cpu-options (CpuOptions ThreadsPerCore); kernel-parameters.txt (nosmt); ABI/testing/sysfs-devices-system-cpu (smt/active, smt/control); Azure compute-optimized Fasv6/Falsv6/Famsv6 and general-purpose Dpsv6/Dplsv6 (no SMT)) (ERGON_HOST_CHECKS=off silences host-check warnings)"

Measured for ergon-io's SQPOLL thread on the loop's sibling against a core of its own (AWS c7i, arrival → decoded, p50 / p99 / max over three rounds): 1 connection 4.0–4.3 / 7.0–11.7 / 35–46 µs against 3.5–3.8 / 8.4–12.9 / 32–118; 10 connections 7.5–8.4 / 29–33 / 96–349 against 5.2–6.0 / 20–24 / 77–109; 100 connections 7.5–7.8 / 32–34 / 95–112 against 5.0–5.3 / 21–22 / 76–301. EKS/EC2: launch template `""CpuOptions"": {""CoreCount"": <the type's cores>, ""ThreadsPerCore"": 1}` (not possible on bare metal, or on T2, C7a, M7a, R7a, Apple silicon Mac or Graviton). Self-managed: boot with `nosmt` (`nosmt=force` cannot be undone at runtime), or `echo off > /sys/devices/system/cpu/smt/control` before the kubelet starts, then delete its cpu_manager_state so it re-pins. Azure: a size without SMT (Fasv6, Falsv6, Famsv6, Dpsv6, Dplsv6: one vCPU is one physical core) (evidence: git show 128747b9^:io/benches/results/flags-aws-20261015T122127Z/tables.md (lines 174-279, arrival → decoded, three rounds); AWS instance-optimize-cpu and instance-specify-cpu-options (CpuOptions ThreadsPerCore); kernel-parameters.txt (nosmt); ABI/testing/sysfs-devices-system-cpu (smt/active, smt/control); Azure compute-optimized Fasv6/Falsv6/Famsv6 and general-purpose Dpsv6/Dplsv6 (no SMT)) (ERGON_HOST_CHECKS=off silences host-check warnings)"

Measured for ergon-io's SQPOLL thread on the loop's sibling against a core of its own (AWS c7i, arrival → decoded, p50 / p99 / max over three rounds): 1 connection 4.0–4.3 / 7.0–11.7 / 35–46 µs against 3.5–3.8 / 8.4–12.9 / 32–118; 10 connections 7.5–8.4 / 29–33 / 96–349 against 5.2–6.0 / 20–24 / 77–109; 100 connections 7.5–7.8 / 32–34 / 95–112 against 5.0–5.3 / 21–22 / 76–301. Other sibling loads are not measured. Turn SMT off on the NODE. EKS/EC2: launch template `""CpuOptions"": {""CoreCount"": <the type's cores>, ""ThreadsPerCore"": 1}` (not possible on bare metal, or on T2, C7a, M7a, R7a, Apple silicon Mac or Graviton). Self-managed: boot with `nosmt` (`nosmt=force` cannot be undone at runtime), or `echo off > /sys/devices/system/cpu/smt/control` before the kubelet starts, then delete its cpu_manager_state so it re-pins. Azure: a size without SMT (Fasv6, Falsv6, Famsv6, Dpsv6, Dplsv6: one vCPU is one physical core) (evidence: git show 128747b9^:io/benches/results/flags-aws-20261015T122127Z/tables.md (lines 174-279, arrival → decoded, three rounds); AWS instance-optimize-cpu and instance-specify-cpu-options (CpuOptions ThreadsPerCore); kernel-parameters.txt (nosmt); ABI/testing/sysfs-devices-system-cpu (smt/active, smt/control); Azure compute-optimized Fasv6/Falsv6/Famsv6 and general-purpose Dpsv6/Dplsv6 (no SMT)) (ERGON_HOST_CHECKS=off silences host-check warnings)"

7,hostcheck,isolated:dpdk@THP=never,a,warning,"T10 warn: SMT is on: on x86 a Kubernetes `cpu: 2` is one physical core, not two, and a busy sibling slows the loop. Measured for ergon-io's SQPOLL thread on the loop's sibling against a core of its own (AWS c7i, arrival → decoded, p50 / p99 / max over three rounds): 1 connection 4.0–4.3 / 7.0–11.7 / 35–46 µs against 3.5–3.8 / 8.4–12.9 / 32–118; 10 connections 7.5–8.4 / 29–33 / 96–349 against 5.2–6.0 / 20–24 / 77–109; 100 connections 7.5–7.8 / 32–34 / 95–112 against 5.0–5.3 / 21–22 / 76–301. Other sibling loads are not measured. Turn SMT off on the NODE. EKS/EC2: launch template `""CpuOptions"": {""CoreCount"": <the type's cores>, ""ThreadsPerCore"": 1}` (not possible on bare metal, or on T2, C7a, M7a, R7a, Apple silicon Mac or Graviton). Self-managed: boot with `nosmt` (`nosmt=force` cannot be undone at runtime), or `echo off > /sys/devices/system/cpu/smt/control` before the kubelet starts, then delete its cpu_manager_state so it re-pins. Azure: a size without SMT (Fasv6, Falsv6, Famsv6, Dpsv6, Dplsv6: one vCPU is one physical core) (evidence: git show 128747b9^:io/benches/results/flags-aws-20261015T122127Z/tables.md (lines 174-279, arrival → decoded, three rounds); AWS instance-optimize-cpu and instance-specify-cpu-options (CpuOptions ThreadsPerCore); kernel-parameters.txt (nosmt); ABI/testing/sysfs-devices-system-cpu (smt/active, smt/control); Azure compute-optimized Fasv6/Falsv6/Famsv6 and general-purpose Dpsv6/Dplsv6 (no SMT)) (ERGON_HOST_CHECKS=off silences host-check warnings)"

EKS/EC2: launch template `""CpuOptions"": {""CoreCount"": <the type's cores>, ""ThreadsPerCore"": 1}` (not possible on bare metal, or on T2, C7a, M7a, R7a, Apple silicon Mac or Graviton). Self-managed: boot with `nosmt` (`nosmt=force` cannot be undone at runtime), or `echo off > /sys/devices/system/cpu/smt/control` before the kubelet starts, then delete its cpu_manager_state so it re-pins. Azure: a size without SMT (Fasv6, Falsv6, Famsv6, Dpsv6, Dplsv6: one vCPU is one physical core) (evidence: git show 128747b9^:io/benches/results/flags-aws-20261015T122127Z/tables.md (lines 174-279, arrival → decoded, three rounds); AWS instance-optimize-cpu and instance-specify-cpu-options (CpuOptions ThreadsPerCore); kernel-parameters.txt (nosmt); ABI/testing/sysfs-devices-system-cpu (smt/active, smt/control); Azure compute-optimized Fasv6/Falsv6/Famsv6 and general-purpose Dpsv6/Dplsv6 (no SMT)) (ERGON_HOST_CHECKS=off silences host-check warnings)"

Measured for ergon-io's SQPOLL thread on the loop's sibling against a core of its own (AWS c7i, arrival → decoded, p50 / p99 / max over three rounds): 1 connection 4.0–4.3 / 7.0–11.7 / 35–46 µs against 3.5–3.8 / 8.4–12.9 / 32–118; 10 connections 7.5–8.4 / 29–33 / 96–349 against 5.2–6.0 / 20–24 / 77–109; 100 connections 7.5–7.8 / 32–34 / 95–112 against 5.0–5.3 / 21–22 / 76–301. Self-managed: boot with `nosmt` (`nosmt=force` cannot be undone at runtime), or `echo off > /sys/devices/system/cpu/smt/control` before the kubelet starts, then delete its cpu_manager_state so it re-pins. EKS/EC2: launch template `""CpuOptions"": {""CoreCount"": <the type's cores>, ""ThreadsPerCore"": 1}` (not possible on bare metal, or on T2, C7a, M7a, R7a, Apple silicon Mac or Graviton). Self-managed: boot with `nosmt` (`nosmt=force` cannot be undone at runtime), or `echo off > /sys/devices/system/cpu/smt/control` before the kubelet starts, then delete its cpu_manager_state so it re-pins. Azure: a size without SMT (Fasv6, Falsv6, Famsv6, Dpsv6, Dplsv6: one vCPU is one physical core) (evidence: git show 128747b9^:io/benches/results/flags-aws-20261015T122127Z/tables.md (lines 174-279, arrival → decoded, three rounds); AWS instance-optimize-cpu and instance-specify-cpu-options (CpuOptions ThreadsPerCore); kernel-parameters.txt (nosmt); ABI/testing/sysfs-devices-system-cpu (smt/active, smt/control); Azure compute-optimized Fasv6/Falsv6/Famsv6 and general-purpose Dpsv6/Dplsv6 (no SMT)) (ERGON_HOST_CHECKS=off silences host-check warnings)"

Measured for ergon-io's SQPOLL thread on the loop's sibling against a core of its own (AWS c7i, arrival → decoded, p50 / p99 / max over three rounds): 1 connection 4.0–4.3 / 7.0–11.7 / 35–46 µs against 3.5–3.8 / 8.4–12.9 / 32–118; 10 connections 7.5–8.4 / 29–33 / 96–349 against 5.2–6.0 / 20–24 / 77–109; 100 connections 7.5–7.8 / 32–34 / 95–112 against 5.0–5.3 / 21–22 / 76–301. Other sibling loads are not measured. Turn SMT off on the NODE. EKS/EC2: launch template `""CpuOptions"": {""CoreCount"": <the type's cores>, ""ThreadsPerCore"": 1}` (not possible on bare metal, or on T2, C7a, M7a, R7a, Apple silicon Mac or Graviton). Self-managed: boot with `nosmt` (`nosmt=force` cannot be undone at runtime), or `echo off > /sys/devices/system/cpu/smt/control` before the kubelet starts, then delete its cpu_manager_state so it re-pins. Azure: a size without SMT (Fasv6, Falsv6, Famsv6, Dpsv6, Dplsv6: one vCPU is one physical core) (evidence: git show 128747b9^:io/benches/results/flags-aws-20261015T122127Z/tables.md (lines 174-279, arrival → decoded, three rounds); AWS instance-optimize-cpu and instance-specify-cpu-options (CpuOptions ThreadsPerCore); kernel-parameters.txt (nosmt); ABI/testing/sysfs-devices-system-cpu (smt/active, smt/control); Azure compute-optimized Fasv6/Falsv6/Famsv6 and general-purpose Dpsv6/Dplsv6 (no SMT)) (ERGON_HOST_CHECKS=off silences host-check warnings)"

Measured for ergon-io's SQPOLL thread on the loop's sibling against a core of its own (AWS c7i, arrival → decoded, p50 / p99 / max over three rounds): 1 connection 4.0–4.3 / 7.0–11.7 / 35–46 µs against 3.5–3.8 / 8.4–12.9 / 32–118; 10 connections 7.5–8.4 / 29–33 / 96–349 against 5.2–6.0 / 20–24 / 77–109; 100 connections 7.5–7.8 / 32–34 / 95–112 against 5.0–5.3 / 21–22 / 76–301. Other sibling loads are not measured. Turn SMT off on the NODE. EKS/EC2: launch template `""CpuOptions"": {""CoreCount"": <the type's cores>, ""ThreadsPerCore"": 1}` (not possible on bare metal, or on T2, C7a, M7a, R7a, Apple silicon Mac or Graviton). Self-managed: boot with `nosmt` (`nosmt=force` cannot be undone at runtime), or `echo off > /sys/devices/system/cpu/smt/control` before the kubelet starts, then delete its cpu_manager_state so it re-pins. Azure: a size without SMT (Fasv6, Falsv6, Famsv6, Dpsv6, Dplsv6: one vCPU is one physical core) (evidence: git show 128747b9^:io/benches/results/flags-aws-20261015T122127Z/tables.md (lines 174-279, arrival → decoded, three rounds); AWS instance-optimize-cpu and instance-specify-cpu-options (CpuOptions ThreadsPerCore); kernel-parameters.txt (nosmt); ABI/testing/sysfs-devices-system-cpu (smt/active, smt/control); Azure compute-optimized Fasv6/Falsv6/Famsv6 and general-purpose Dpsv6/Dplsv6 (no SMT)) (ERGON_HOST_CHECKS=off silences host-check warnings)"
